{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01S5kXh1L7FvGCnpAeWr6nUu","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# D-0061 — No-Shared-State Cross-Trial Integration Test (NFR-ISO1)\n2\t\n3\t**Roadmap entry**: R-061\n4\t**Task**: T03.20\n5\t**Component(s) under test**: COMP-003 `RunOrchestrator` × COMP-006 `HomeIsolation`\n6\t**Source**: `tests/cli/eval/test_no_shared_state.py`\n7\t**Non-functional requirement**: NFR-ISO1 — \"No shared HOMEs, no shared file\n8\thandles (e.g. `auggie-first.jsonl`), and no port collisions at\n9\t`--parallel 15`. Tests run N×15 trials.\"\n10\t\n11\t## Purpose\n12\t\n13\tT03.16 (D-0058) already proves that a *single* parallel run of 15 evals\n14\tyields per-eval isolated HOMEs, session_ids, and telemetry namespaces.\n15\tWhat T03.16 cannot detect is a class of regressions that only surface\n16\t*across* runs:\n17\t\n18\t- A module-level dict that caches `mkdtemp` outputs by `eval_id` and\n19\t  returns the stale path on the next trial.\n20\t- A UUID generator seeded once per process so trial 1's session_ids\n21\t  re-appear in trial 2.\n22\t- A JSONL writer that holds a file handle open across runs and fans new\n23\t  writes into the previous trial's file.\n24\t- A future port allocator that binds at `setup` time and re-binds the\n25\t  same port in the next trial because the registry is never cleared.\n26\t\n27\tT03.20 closes that gap by iterating the same composition `N=3` times\n28\tagainst a **single shared scratch root** and asserting cross-trial\n29\tuniqueness pairwise across all `3 × 15 = 45` evals.\n30\t\n31\t## Integration scenario\n32\t\n33\t### Wiring\n34\t\n35\t```\n36\ttrial in 0..N-1:\n37\t    spec list (15)\n38\t        │\n39\t        └─→ RunOrchestrator(run_one=isolation_worker_t{trial}, cancellation_token=None)\n40\t                  │\n41\t                  └─→ ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=clamp(parallel=8, [1,15]))\n42\t                          │\n43\t                          └─→ isolation_worker(spec):\n44\t                                  HomeIsolation(eval_id=spec.id,\n45\t                                                home_root=SHARED_scratch_root,\n46\t                                                session_id=f\"sess-t{trial}-{spec.id}\")\n47\t                                      │\n48\t                                      ├─ setup(config=permissive_config)\n49\t                                      │     → mkdtemp({eval_id}-…) under home_root\n50\t                                      ├─ env()\n51\t                                      │     → {HOME, CLAUDE_SESSION_ID}\n52\t                                      ├─ <write telemetry.jsonl in home_path/.eval-logs/>\n53\t There is no orchestrator-layer retry either.\n45\t\n46\t## Re-running a failed eval — the `--eval` subset path\n47\t\n48\tAfter a run completes, the Reporter writes `summary.{md,json}` to the\n49\trun directory. Inspect the table to identify failed eval ids, then\n50\tre-run only those evals:\n51\t\n52\t```bash\n53\t# Full suite run (deterministic single pass)\n54\tsuperclaude eval run suites/full.yaml --parallel 8\n55\t\n56\t# Re-run only the failing ids after diagnosis (any number of --eval)\n57\tsuperclaude eval run suites/full.yaml --eval E03 --eval E07\n58\t\n59\t# Quick smoke (3-4 evals); same flag, different subset\n60\tsuperclaude eval run suites/quick.yaml --eval E01 --eval E02\n61\t```\n62\t\n63\tNotes:\n64\t\n65\t* `--eval` accepts the **expanded** id (post-parameterize) so a\n66\t  parameterized eval like `E07[case=a]` is targetable by its full\n67\t  identifier. An id that does not match any expanded eval exits with\n68\t  code 2 (see `tests/cli/eval/test_eval_id_regex.py` and\n69\t  `src/superclaude/cli/eval/commands.py` — FR-CLI3).\n70\t* Subset runs are independent processes — they allocate a fresh\n71\t  `HomeIsolation` per eval and emit a fresh `summary.{md,json}` under\n72\t  their own run directory. There is no shared state with the originating\n73\t  run.\n74\t* `--keep-home` controls whether failed HOMEs survive the second run\n75\t  the same way it does for the first. Default behaviour preserves\n76\t  failed HOMEs and discards successful ones; the `--keep-home` flag\n77\t  forces preservation for both.\n78\t\n79\t## When *will* retries happen? On the first observed failure that matches the MCP-flaky taxonomy,\n87\t   the runner re-executes the lifecycle exactly once.\n88\t3. The second result is the recorded outcome; the first attempt is\n89\t   preserved in the per-eval JSONL log under a `retry_attempt`\n90\t   namespace.\n91\t\n92\tThis branch is **deferred** until OQ-10 closes and the exact taxonomy\n93\tis empirically resolved (see\n94\t`.dev/releases/current/cliEval/decisions.md §B`). A\n  failing `ExpectResult` produces a `FAIL` outcome; a harness exception\n  produces `ERRORED`. Neither path re-enters the lifecycle.\n* The orchestrator (`RunOrchestrator`, T03.15) collects one\n  `EvalOutcome` per submitted future via\n  `concurrent.futures.as_completed` and writes it through to the\n  Reporter. There is no orchestrator-layer retry either.\n\n## Re-running a failed eval — the `--eval` subset path\n\nAfter a run completes, the Reporter writes `summary.{md,json}` to the\nrun directory. Inspect the table to identify failed eval ids, then\nre-run only those evals:\n\n```bash\n# Full suite run (deterministic single pass)\nsuperclaude eval run suites/full.yaml --parallel 8\n\n# Re-run only the failing ids after diagnosis (any number of --eval)\nsuperclaude eval run suites/full.yaml --eval E03 --eval E07\n\n# Quick smoke (3-4 evals); same flag, different subset\nsuperclaude eval run suites/quick.yaml --eval E01 --eval E02\n```\n\nNotes:\n\n* `--eval` accepts the **expanded** id (post-parameterize) so a\n  parameterized eval like `E07[case=a]` is targetable by its full\n  identifier. An id that does not match any expanded eval exits with\n  code 2 (see `tests/cli/eval/test_eval_id_regex.py` and\n  `src/superclaude/cli/eval/commands.py` — FR-CLI3).\n* Subset runs are independent processes — they allocate a fresh\n  `HomeIsolation` per eval and emit a fresh `summary.{md,json}` under\n  their own run directory. There is no shared state with the originating\n  run.\n* `--keep-home` controls whether failed HOMEs survive the second run\n  the same way it does for the first. Default behaviour preserves\n  failed HOMEs and discards successful ones; the `--keep-home` flag\n  forces preservation for both.\n\n## When *will* retries happen? (OQ-10 / R3-mit / T05.23)\n\nThe roadmap reserves one in-process retry for evals whose failure mode\nmatches **MCP server flakiness** (timeout, unreachable) — never for\nassertion failures, never for harness exceptions. The intended UX is:\n\n1. The manifest tags the eval with the future `MCP-flaky` tag.\n2. On the first observed failure that matches the MCP-flaky taxonomy,\n   the runner re-executes the lifecycle exactly once.\n3. The second result is the recorded outcome; the first attempt is\n   preserved in the per-eval JSONL log under a `retry_attempt`\n   namespace.\n\nThis branch is **deferred** until OQ-10 closes and the exact taxonomy\nis empirically resolved (see\n`.dev/releases/current/cliEval/decisions.md §B`). Until then, the\n`MCP_FLAKY_TAG = \"MCP-flaky\"` constant on `EvalRunner` reserves the\nexact tag string the future implementation will look for; tagging a\nmanifest entry with it today is a no-op.\n\n## Operator-facing invariants\n\n* `EvalRunner.DEFAULT_RETRY_COUNT` is the canonical pin. Reading it from\n  a script (e.g. CI safety check) lets a deployment confirm the\n  harness build has retries disabled.\n* `EvalRunner.MCP_FLAKY_TAG` is the canonical pin for the future\n  retry-once tag string. Manifest tooling can reference it instead of\n  hard-coding `\"MCP-flaky\"`.\n* Any change to either constant requires an updated design-spec entry\n  and an explicit M3/M5 closure record per the OQ-10 decision row.\n\n## See also\n\n* `src/superclaude/cli/eval/runner.py` — `EvalRunner` class body,\n  `DEFAULT_RETRY_COUNT` and `MCP_FLAKY_TAG`.\n* `tests/cli/eval/test_retry_policy.py` — regression suite locking the\n  contract in place.\n* `.dev/releases/current/cliEval/design-spec.md §13` — Bounded retry\n  contract.\n* `.dev/releases/current/cliEval/decisions.md §B` — OQ-10 deferral\n  record (target M3/M5).\n* `.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0051/spec.md` — Per-task\n  deliverable record for T03.08.\n","numLines":122,"startLine":1,"totalLines":122}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_011KwDLsjY46es2K2xxv4eFT","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"COMP-003 RunOrchestrator — parallel eval scheduler.\n2\t\n3\tTask T03.15 / Deliverable D-0057 / Roadmap row R-057 (COMP-003).\n4\t\n5\tThe orchestrator is the single component that turns a sequence of\n6\texpanded :class:`EvalSpec` rows into a sequence of :class:`EvalOutcome`\n7\trows by scheduling per-spec :class:`EvalRunner` workers through\n8\t``concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor + as_completed`` — the AC6\n9\tpattern the design-spec pins for cliEval. The implementation deliberately\n10\tmirrors the shape used by ``src/superclaude/cli/prd/executor.py:774-802``\n11\tso a reader switching between the PRD pipeline and the eval harness sees\n12\tthe same scheduling skeleton.\n13\t\n14\tResponsibilities\n15\t================\n16\t\n17\tThe orchestrator owns three cross-cutting concerns the lifecycle layer\n18\tintentionally defers:\n19\t\n20\t1. **Concurrency budget.** ``max_workers`` is clamped to ``[1, 15]`` per\n21\t   design-spec §11 (NFR-PERF2) with a default of ``8``. Values outside\n22\t   the range either raise (``parallel < 1``) or saturate to the upper\n23\t   bound (``parallel > 15``). The clamp is enforced once at the top of\n24\t   :meth:`RunOrchestrator.run` so the rest of the body can trust the\n25\t   effective value.\n26\t2. **Outcome contract.** :class:`RunOrchestrator` guarantees one\n27\t   :class:`EvalOutcome` per input :class:`EvalSpec` in the original\n28\t   sequence order. The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) enforces the\n29\t   N'-vs-K invariant on top of this — the orchestrator never elides a\n30\t   spec.\n31\t3. **Cooperative cancellation.** When a shared\n32\t   :class:`CancellationToken` is wired (NFR-REL1 / T03.07), the\n33\t   orchestrator stops submitting new work as soon as it observes the\n34\t   token cancelled. Specs that were never submitted synthesise an\n35\t   ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome at the orchestrator layer so the per-spec\n36\t   contract still holds.  In-flight runners observe the same token via\n37\t   :class:`EvalRunner` and convert worker-thread\n38\t   ``KeyboardInterrupt`` / ``SystemExit`` into ``INTERRUPTED`` outcomes\n39\t   themselves — the orchestrator never has to translate an exception\n40\t   into a status.\n41\t\n42\tThe orchestrator deliberately does *not* install signal handlers. Signal\n43\tinstallation is the CLI dispatcher's job (Phase 4): the dispatcher\n44\tallocates a :class:`CancellationToken`, wraps the orchestrator call in a\n45\t:class:`SignalHandlerInstaller`, and hands the token down so the eval\n46\trunners observe the same cancellation flag the signal handler flips.\n47\t\n48\tWorker contract\n49\t===============\n50\t\n51\tThe orchestrator does not construct :class:`EvalRunner` instances\n52\titself. Building an :class:`EvalRunner` requires per-eval HOME\n53\tisolation, scratch paths, executor wiring, and an expect-callable\n54\tsequence — none of which the orchestrator can resolve. Instead the\n55\tcaller supplies a ``run_one: Callable[[EvalSpec], EvalOutcome]`` worker\n56\tthat wraps everything from ``HomeIsolation`` allocation through\n57\t:meth:`EvalRunner.run` into a single closure. Production callers will\n58\ttypically write::\n59\t\n60\t    def run_one(spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n61\t        home = HomeIsolation(eval_id=spec.id, ...)\n62\t        runner = EvalRunner(home=home, ...)\n63\t        return runner.run(spec)\n64\t\n65\tThis indirection keeps the orchestrator a pure scheduling primitive and\n66\tmakes unit-testing trivial — tests substitute a stub worker that returns\n67\ta canned outcome without touching the filesystem.\n68\t\n69\tIf the worker raises (e.g. construction failure, unexpected propagation\n70\tof ``KeyboardInterrupt`` because the caller declined to wire a token),\n71\tthe orchestrator folds the exception into an ``ERRORED`` outcome\n72\trecording the fully-qualified exception classname — the same shape\n73\t:func:`run_eval` (T03.04) emits when a harness step fails. This keeps\n74\tthe per-spec contract intact even when a worker misbehaves.\n75\t\"\"\"\n76\t\n77\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n78\t\n79\tfrom concurrent.futures import Future, ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed\n80\tfrom typing import Callable, ClassVar, Optional, Sequence\n81\t\n82\tfrom .disk_budget import DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON, DiskBudgetPoller\n83\tfrom .models import EvalOutcome, EvalSpec\n84\tfrom .signal_handler import CancellationToken\n85\t\n86\t__all__ = [\"RunOrchestrator\", \"EvalWorker\"]\n87\t\n88\t\n89\t# A worker callable receives an :class:`EvalSpec` and returns the\n90\t# corresponding :class:`EvalOutcome`. Production wiring composes\n91\t# ``EvalRunner.run`` with the per-spec resource allocation the\n92\t# orchestrator deliberately does not own.\n93\tEvalWorker = Callable[[EvalSpec], EvalOutcome]\n94\t\n95\t\n96\tclass RunOrchestrator:\n97\t    \"\"\"Schedule per-spec eval workers in parallel via ``ThreadPoolExecutor``.\n98\t\n99\t    Parameters\n100\t    ----------\n101\t    run_one:\n102\t        Worker callable invoked once per :class:`EvalSpec`. Returns the\n103\t        :class:`EvalOutcome` for that spec. Worker exceptions are folded\n104\t        into an ``ERRORED`` outcome — the orchestrator never re-raises\n105\t        out of :meth:`run`.\n106\t    cancellation_token:\n107\t        Optional :class:`CancellationToken` shared with the CLI\n108\t        dispatcher's signal handler (NFR-REL1 / T03.07). When the token\n109\t        is set, the orchestrator stops submitting new work and\n110\t        synthesises an ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome for every spec that was\n111\t        never scheduled.\n112\t    disk_budget_poller:\n113\t        Optional :class:`DiskBudgetPoller` enforcing the NFR-PERF4\n114\t        ``--max-disk-mb`` budget (T03.19 / D-0060 / R-060). When wired,\n115\t        the orchestrator starts the poller before submission, checks\n116\t        :meth:`DiskBudgetPoller.is_breached` alongside the cancellation\n117\t        token between submissions, and synthesises a ``SKIPPED`` outcome\n118\t        with ``skip_reason=\"disk_budget_exceeded\"`` for every spec that\n119\t        was never scheduled after a breach. In-flight workers run to\n120\t        completion — the poller never interrupts a thread. A disabled\n121\t        poller (``max_disk_mb=0``) is silently no-op'd by\n122\t        :meth:`DiskBudgetPoller.start`, so the orchestrator can be wired\n123\t        with a poller unconditionally.\n124\t    \"\"\"\n125\t\n126\t    DEFAULT_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 8\n127\t    MIN_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 1\n128\t    MAX_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 15\n129\t\n130\t    def __init__(\n131\t        self,\n132\t        *,\n133\t        run_one: EvalWorker,\n134\t        cancellation_token: Optional[CancellationToken] = None,\n135\t        disk_budget_poller: Optional[DiskBudgetPoller] = None,\n136\t    ) -> None:\n137\t        if not callable(run_one):\n138\t            raise TypeError(\"run_one must be callable\")\n139\t        self._run_one = run_one\n140\t        self._cancellation_token = cancellation_token\n141\t        self._disk_budget_poller = disk_budget_poller\n142\t\n143\t    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n144\t    # Public surface\n145\t    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n146\t\n147\t    def run(\n148\t        self,\n149\t        specs: Sequence[EvalSpec],\n150\t        *,\n151\t        parallel: int = DEFAULT_PARALLEL,\n152\t    ) -> list[EvalOutcome]:\n153\t        \"\"\"Execute every spec in parallel and return one outcome per spec.\n154\t\n155\t        Parameters\n156\t        ----------\n157\t        specs:\n158\t            Expanded :class:`EvalSpec` sequence (post FR-EXP1 expansion).\n159\t            The returned list preserves this order so the Reporter\n160\t            renders rows deterministically.\n161\t        parallel:\n162\t            Requested concurrency level. Clamped to\n163\t            ``[MIN_PARALLEL, MAX_PARALLEL]``; values below the minimum\n164\t            raise :class:`ValueError`, values above the maximum saturate\n165\t            to ``MAX_PARALLEL``. Defaults to :attr:`DEFAULT_PARALLEL`.\n166\t\n167\t        Returns\n168\t        -------\n169\t        list[EvalOutcome]\n170\t            One :class:`EvalOutcome` per input spec, in the input order.\n171\t        \"\"\"\n172\t\n173\t        # The minimum-bound rejection is the only hard failure: a\n174\t        # negative or zero concurrency value is almost certainly a\n175\t        # caller bug (CLI argument parsing should have caught it\n176\t        # earlier) and silently coercing it would mask the bug. We\n258\t                        # only get here when the worker itself raised\n259\t                        # (construction failure, unwired-token\n260\t                        # KeyboardInterrupt propagation, etc.). The implementation deliberately\nmirrors the shape used by ``src/superclaude/cli/prd/executor.py:774-802``\nso a reader switching between the PRD pipeline and the eval harness sees\nthe same scheduling skeleton.\n\nResponsibilities\n================\n\nThe orchestrator owns three cross-cutting concerns the lifecycle layer\nintentionally defers:\n\n1. **Concurrency budget.** ``max_workers`` is clamped to ``[1, 15]`` per\n   design-spec §11 (NFR-PERF2) with a default of ``8``. Values outside\n   the range either raise (``parallel < 1``) or saturate to the upper\n   bound (``parallel > 15``). The clamp is enforced once at the top of\n   :meth:`RunOrchestrator.run` so the rest of the body can trust the\n   effective value.\n2. **Outcome contract.** :class:`RunOrchestrator` guarantees one\n   :class:`EvalOutcome` per input :class:`EvalSpec` in the original\n   sequence order. The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) enforces the\n   N'-vs-K invariant on top of this — the orchestrator never elides a\n   spec.\n3. **Cooperative cancellation.** When a shared\n   :class:`CancellationToken` is wired (NFR-REL1 / T03.07), the\n   orchestrator stops submitting new work as soon as it observes the\n   token cancelled. Specs that were never submitted synthesise an\n   ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome at the orchestrator layer so the per-spec\n   contract still holds.  In-flight runners observe the same token via\n   :class:`EvalRunner` and convert worker-thread\n   ``KeyboardInterrupt`` / ``SystemExit`` into ``INTERRUPTED`` outcomes\n   themselves — the orchestrator never has to translate an exception\n   into a status.\n\nThe orchestrator deliberately does *not* install signal handlers. Signal\ninstallation is the CLI dispatcher's job (Phase 4): the dispatcher\nallocates a :class:`CancellationToken`, wraps the orchestrator call in a\n:class:`SignalHandlerInstaller`, and hands the token down so the eval\nrunners observe the same cancellation flag the signal handler flips.\n\nWorker contract\n===============\n\nThe orchestrator does not construct :class:`EvalRunner` instances\nitself. Building an :class:`EvalRunner` requires per-eval HOME\nisolation, scratch paths, executor wiring, and an expect-callable\nsequence — none of which the orchestrator can resolve. Instead the\ncaller supplies a ``run_one: Callable[[EvalSpec], EvalOutcome]`` worker\nthat wraps everything from ``HomeIsolation`` allocation through\n:meth:`EvalRunner.run` into a single closure. Production callers will\ntypically write::\n\n    def run_one(spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n        home = HomeIsolation(eval_id=spec.id, ...)\n        runner = EvalRunner(home=home, ...)\n        return runner.run(spec)\n\nThis indirection keeps the orchestrator a pure scheduling primitive and\nmakes unit-testing trivial — tests substitute a stub worker that returns\na canned outcome without touching the filesystem.\n\nIf the worker raises (e.g. construction failure, unexpected propagation\nof ``KeyboardInterrupt`` because the caller declined to wire a token),\nthe orchestrator folds the exception into an ``ERRORED`` outcome\nrecording the fully-qualified exception classname — the same shape\n:func:`run_eval` (T03.04) emits when a harness step fails. This keeps\nthe per-spec contract intact even when a worker misbehaves.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom concurrent.futures import Future, ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed\nfrom typing import Callable, ClassVar, Optional, Sequence\n\nfrom .disk_budget import DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON, DiskBudgetPoller\nfrom .models import EvalOutcome, EvalSpec\nfrom .signal_handler import CancellationToken\n\n__all__ = [\"RunOrchestrator\", \"EvalWorker\"]\n\n\n# A worker callable receives an :class:`EvalSpec` and returns the\n# corresponding :class:`EvalOutcome`. Already-running workers observe\n                    # the token themselves (via :class:`EvalRunner`) and\n                    # convert to INTERRUPTED outcomes; we synthesise an\n                    # INTERRUPTED row for every spec that did not even\n                    # reach the pool so the per-spec contract still holds.\n                    #\n                    # Cancellation is checked *before* the disk-budget\n                    # breach so a SIGINT received concurrently with a\n                    # breach routes the spec to INTERRUPTED (operator\n                    # intent dominates a resource limit).\n                    if self._is_cancelled():\n                        cancelled_indices.append(index)\n                        continue\n                    if self._is_disk_budget_exceeded():\n                        disk_budget_skipped_indices.append(index)\n                        continue\n                    future = pool.submit(self._invoke_worker, spec)\n                    futures[future] = index\n\n                # Drain the pool. ``as_completed`` is the AC6 idiom — it\n                # yields each future the instant its worker returns,\n                # letting downstream readers (telemetry, TUI) update in\n                # near real-time without waiting for the slowest eval.\n                # In-flight workers continue to completion even after a\n                # disk-budget breach — the AC6 contract requires \"in-\n                # flight evals complete\" before the run exits.\n                for future in as_completed(futures):\n                    index = futures[future]\n                    spec = specs[index]\n                    try:\n                        outcomes[index] = future.result()\n                    except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — see docstring\n                        # The worker should fold its own harness errors\n                        # into an ERRORED outcome via :func:`run_eval`. We\n                        # only get here when the worker itself raised\n                        # (construction failure, unwired-token\n                        # KeyboardInterrupt propagation, etc.). Translate\n                        # to ERRORED so :meth:`run` never re-raises.\n                        outcomes[index] = self._errored_outcome(spec, exc)\n        finally:\n            if poller is not None:\n                poller.stop()\n\n        # Backfill INTERRUPTED outcomes for never-submitted specs after\n        # the pool has shut down so we cannot race with an in-flight\n        # cancellation.\n        for index in cancelled_indices:\n            outcomes[index] = self._interrupted_outcome(specs[index])\n        # Backfill SKIPPED outcomes for specs we declined to submit\n        # because the disk budget was breached. ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n411\t\n412\t\n413\tclass TestParallelClampAtFifteen:\n414\t    def test_parallel_sixteen_clamps_to_fifteen(\n415\t        self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n416\t    ) -> None:\n417\t        \"\"\"``parallel=16`` saturates to 15; no worker observes a 16th peer.\"\"\"\n418\t\n419\t        observed_concurrency: list[int] = []\n420\t        concurrency_lock = threading.Lock()\n421\t        isolations_seen: dict[str, HomeIsolation] = {}\n422\t        isolations_lock = threading.Lock()\n423\t\n424\t        # Use a hold-event to keep every worker alive until the test\n425\t        # releases them, so the concurrency snapshot has a chance to\n426\t        # observe the upper-bound peer count. Without the barrier the\n427\t        # workers complete fast enough that the snapshot under-counts.\n428\t        hold_event = threading.Event()\n429\t        worker = _make_isolation_worker(\n430\t            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n431\t            config=eval_config,\n432\t            observed_concurrency=observed_concurrency,\n433\t            concurrency_lock=concurrency_lock,\n434\t            isolations_seen=isolations_seen,\n435\t            isolations_lock=isolations_lock,\n436\t            hold_event=hold_event,\n437\t        )\n438\t\n439\t        # Submit 20 specs at parallel=16; if the clamp leaks we'll\n440\t        # observe more than 15 workers running concurrently. Once the\n441\t        # peak is observed we release the barrier so the pool drains.\n442\t        specs = _build_specs(20)\n443\t        orch = RunOrchestrator(run_one=worker)\n444\t\n445\t        # Release the barrier on a delay so workers actually pile up\n446\t        # before any of them finish. The delay is generous (1s) but\n447\t        # bounded so a hung worker cannot block the test indefinitely.\n448\t        releaser = threading.Timer(1.0, hold_event.set)\n449\t        releaser.start()\n450\t        try:\n451\t            outcomes = orch.run(specs, parallel=16)\n452\t        finally:\n453\t            hold_event.set()  # unconditionally release in case of failure\n454\t            releaser.cancel()\n455\t\n456\t        assert len(outcomes) == 20\n457\t        # The clamp is the *only* thing that can keep observed_concurrency\n458\t        # bounded at 15: ``ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=16)`` would\n459\t        # have admitted a 16th worker before any of them finished.\n460\t        assert max(observed_concurrency) <= RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL, (\n461\t            f\"observed concurrency peaked at {max(observed_concurrency)} \"\n462\t            f\"workers;            assert event[\"env_home\"] == event[\"home_path\"]\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# AC: parallel=16 clamps to MAX_PARALLEL=15\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\nclass TestParallelClampAtFifteen:\n    def test_parallel_sixteen_clamps_to_fifteen(\n        self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"``parallel=16`` saturates to 15; no worker observes a 16th peer.\"\"\"\n\n        observed_concurrency: list[int] = []\n        concurrency_lock = threading.Lock()\n        isolations_seen: dict[str, HomeIsolation] = {}\n        isolations_lock = threading.Lock()\n\n        # Use a hold-event to keep every worker alive until the test\n        # releases them, so the concurrency snapshot has a chance to\n        # observe the upper-bound peer count. Without the barrier the\n        # workers complete fast enough that the snapshot under-counts.\n        hold_event = threading.Event()\n        worker = _make_isolation_worker(\n            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n            config=eval_config,\n            observed_concurrency=observed_concurrency,\n            concurrency_lock=concurrency_lock,\n            isolations_seen=isolations_seen,\n            isolations_lock=isolations_lock,\n            hold_event=hold_event,\n        )\n\n        # Submit 20 specs at parallel=16; if the clamp leaks we'll\n        # observe more than 15 workers running concurrently. Once the\n        # peak is observed we release the barrier so the pool drains.\n        specs = _build_specs(20)\n        orch = RunOrchestrator(run_one=worker)\n\n        # Release the barrier on a delay so workers actually pile up\n        # before any of them finish. The delay is generous (1s) but\n        # bounded so a hung worker cannot block the test indefinitely.\n        releaser = threading.Timer(1.0, hold_event.set)\n        releaser.start()\n        try:\n            outcomes = orch.run(specs, parallel=16)\n        finally:\n            hold_event.set()  # unconditionally release in case of failure\n            releaser.cancel()\n\n        assert len(outcomes) == 20\n        # The clamp is the *only* thing that can keep observed_concurrency\n        # bounded at 15: ``ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=16)`` would\n        # have admitted a 16th worker before any of them finished.\n        assert max(observed_concurrency) <= RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL, (\n            f\"observed concurrency peaked at {max(observed_concurrency)} \"\n            f\"workers; clamp at {RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL} leaked\"\n        )\n        # Sanity check that the test actually achieved parallelism — if\n        # the snapshot peaks at 1 the assertion above is vacuous.\n        assert max(observed_concurrency) > 1, (\n            \"concurrency snapshot is degenerate; clamp assertion is vacuous\"\n        )\n\n    def test_parallel_at_max_admits_fifteen_concurrent_workers(\n        self, scratch_root: Path, eval_config: EvalConfig\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"``parallel=15`` actually admits all 15 workers simultaneously.\"\"\"\n\n        observed_concurrency: list[int] = []\n        concurrency_lock = threading.Lock()\n        isolations_seen: dict[str, HomeIsolation] = {}\n        isolations_lock = threading.Lock()\n\n        hold_event = threading.Event()\n        worker = _make_isolation_worker(\n            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n            config=eval_config,\n            observed_concurrency=observed_concurrency,\n            concurrency_lock=concurrency_lock,\n            isolations_seen=isolations_seen,\n            isolations_lock=isolations_lock,\n            hold_event=hold_event,\n        )\n\n        specs = _build_specs(15)\n        orch = RunOrchestrator(run_one=worker)\n        releaser = threading.Timer(1.0, hold_event.set)\n        releaser.start()\n        try:\n            outcomes = orch.run(specs, parallel=15)\n        finally:\n            hold_event.set()\n            releaser.cancel()\n\n        assert len(outcomes) == 15\n        # Confirms the clamp does not under-admit either: when the\n        # request equals MAX_PARALLEL, all 15 workers should run\n        # concurrently. Without the barrier the snapshot could\n        # under-count by chance; with the 1s hold every spawned worker\n        # is parked in ``hold_event.wait`` long enough to be counted.\n        assert max(observed_concurrency) == RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL, (\n            f\"parallel=15 admitted only {max(observed_concurrency)} \"\n            f\"concurrent workers; expected {RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL}\"\n        )\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Helpers\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _read_telemetry_home(iso: HomeIsolation) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Return the ``home_path`` stamp the worker baked into the JSONL log.\n\n    ``HomeIsolation.teardown(keep=True)`` clears the private ``_home_path``\n    slot so :attr:`HomeIsolation.home_path` raises ``RuntimeError`` after\n    the worker returns. Reading the value from disk closes that gap\n    *and* doubles as a verification that the JSONL was actually written\n    by the worker we expected.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    return _read_telemetry_event(iso)[\"home_path\"]\n\n\ndef _read_telemetry_event(iso: HomeIsolation) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Parse the eval's ``telemetry.jsonl`` and return the first event.\n\n    The file is written exactly once per eval, so the first JSON object\n    is the canonical record of what the worker observed when ``setup``\n    returned.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    # The worker's JSONL lives at ``home_path/.eval-logs/telemetry.jsonl``;\n    # we cannot ask ``iso.home_path`` because teardown(keep=True) cleared\n    # the slot. Instead re-derive the path by scanning ``home_root`` for\n    # the subdir whose name starts with the eval id (``mkdtemp`` prefix\n    # convention in HomeIsolation.setup).\n    home_root = iso.home_root\n    candidates = [\n        p\n        for p in home_root.iterdir()\n        if p.is_dir() and p.name.startswith(f\"{iso.eval_id}-\")\n    ]\n    assert len(candidates) == 1, (\n        f\"expected exactly one preserved HOME for {iso.eval_id} under \"\n        f\"{home_root}; found {[str(c) for c in candidates]}\"\n    )\n    telemetry_path = candidates[0] / \".eval-logs\" / \"telemetry.jsonl\"\n    assert telemetry_path.exists(), (\n        f\"telemetry log missing for {iso.eval_id} at {telemetry_path}\"\n    )\n    with telemetry_path.open(encoding=\"utf-8\") as fh:\n        return json.loads(fh.readline())\n","numLines":560,"startLine":1,"totalLines":560}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01CnC6nHHMg1RthDrvyh7dsX","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# D-0060 — NFR-PERF4 Disk Budget Poller\n2\t\n3\t**Task:** T03.19 (Phase 3, Roadmap R-060)\n4\t**Modules:** `src/superclaude/cli/eval/disk_budget.py` (new), `src/superclaude/cli/eval/orchestrator.py` (integration)\n5\t**Tests:** `tests/cli/eval/test_disk_budget.py`\n6\t**Status:** Implemented 2026-05-20\n7\t\n8\t## 1. Goal\n9\t\n10\tBound the cumulative artifact size under `--output-dir` during a real\n11\teval run so a runaway suite cannot fill the host disk. The cliEval\n12\tdesign-spec §4 / §13 (NFR-PERF4) commit the orchestrator to a five-\n13\tsecond polling cadence and a default 1024 MB budget; design-spec §10\n14\trisk R4 documents the failure mode the poller mitigates (a 15-eval\n15\tfan-out with PTY transcripts can grow past several hundred megabytes\n16\ton a slow eval and corrupt every neighbouring run).\n17\t\n18\tOn breach the harness must:\n19\t\n20\t1. Stop scheduling new evals.\n21\t2. Allow in-flight evals to complete (no thread-kill).\n22\t3. Exit `2` (harness error) with a `disk_budget_exceeded.json` side-car\n23\t   the Reporter surfaces under `RunSummary.artifacts`.\n24\t\n25\t## 2. Public Surface\n26\t\n27\t```python\n28\tfrom superclaude.cli.eval.disk_budget import (\n29\t    DEFAULT_DISK_BUDGET_MB,             # 1024\n30\t    DEFAULT_DISK_POLL_TICK_SEC,         # 5.0\n31\t    DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME, # \"disk_budget_exceeded.json\"\n32\t    DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_EXIT_CODE,     # 2\n33\t    DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON,        # \"disk_budget_exceeded\"\n34\t    BreachDetail,\n35\t    DiskBudgetPoller,\n36\t)\n37\t```\n38\t\n39\t### 2.1 `DiskBudgetPoller`\n40\t\n41\t| Attribute / method | Contract |\n42\t|---|---|\n43\t| `__init__(output_dir, *, max_disk_mb=1024, tick_sec=5.0, artifact_name=..., ensure_output_dir=False, clock=None)` | Construct the poller. `max_disk_mb < 0` → `ValueError`; non-`int` (or `bool`) → `TypeError`; `tick_sec ≤ 0` → `ValueError`. |\n44\t| `enabled` | `True` when `max_disk_mb > 0`. |\n45\t| `max_disk_mb`, `budget_bytes`, `tick_sec`, `output_dir`, `artifact_name` | Read-only configuration accessors. |\n46\t| `is_breached()` | One-shot flag flipped by the polling thread on the first breach. Stays `True` for the lifetime of the poller. |\n47\t| `breach_detail()` | `Mapping[str, object]` matching `BreachDetail.to_dict()`, or `None` while healthy. |\n48\t| `artifact_path()` | `Path` of the side-car file once written, or `None`. |\n49\t| `start()` | Spawn the background daemon thread. No-op when `max_disk_mb == 0` or the thread is already alive. |\n50\t| `stop(*, timeout=None)` | Signal the thread to exit and `join` it. Idempotent. Does **not** clear the breach flag. |\n51\t| `__enter__` / `__exit__` | Convenience context-manager form (`start` on enter, `stop` on exit). |\n52\t\n53\t### 2.2 `BreachDetail`\n54\t\n55\tFrozen dataclass serialised verbatim into the side-car JSON:\n56\t\n57\t| Field | Type | Notes |\n58\t|---|---|---|\n59\t| `reason` | `str` | Always `DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON`. |\n60\t| `output_dir` | `str` | Absolute path of the watched directory. |\n61\t| `usage_bytes` | `int` | Measured cumulative size at the breach tick. |\n62\t| `budget_bytes` | `int` | `max_disk_mb * 1024 * 1024`. |\n63\t| `max_disk_mb` | `int` | Operator-configured budget. |\n64\t| `ticked_at` | `str` | ISO-8601 UTC (`Z`-suffixed) timestamp. |\n65\t\n66\t### 2.3 Side-car shape\n67\t\n68\tWritten to `output_dir / DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME`:\n69\t\n70\t```json\n71\t{\n72\t  \"budget_bytes\": 1073741824,\n73\t  \"max_disk_mb\": 1024,\n74\t  \"output_dir\": \"/tmp/run-xyz\",\n75\t  \"reason\": \"disk_budget_exceeded\",\n76\t  \"ticked_at\": \"2026-05-20T13:28:09Z\",\n77\t  \"usage_bytes\": 1099773888\n78\t}\n79\t```\n80\t\n81\t`json.dumps(..., indent=2, sort_keys=True)` plus a trailing newline so\n82\tthe file is review-friendly and round-trips through `jq`. The Reporter\n83\t(COMP-008) surfaces the path under\n84\t`RunSummary.artifacts[\"disk_budget_exceeded\"]`.\n85\t\n86\t## 3. Polling cadence & breach semantics\n87\t\n88\t```python\n89\twhile not self._stop_event.wait(self._tick_sec):\n90\t    usage_bytes = self._compute_usage()\n91\t    if usage_bytes > self._budget_bytes:\n92\t        self._record_breach(usage_bytes)\n93\t        return\n94\t```\n95\t\n96\t* `threading.Event.wait(tick_sec)` is the cancellable sleep — `stop()`\n97\t  short-circuits the next tick instead of waiting up to `tick_sec` for\n98\t  the loop to notice.\n99\t* First tick fires *after* the initial sleep so an empty run directory\n100\t  does not trip the breach during start-up.\n101\t* `_compute_usage` walks the output tree with `rglob('*')`, skips\n102\t  symlinks (cannot be tricked into double-counting via a sibling-tree\n103\t  link), and swallows `OSError` / `FileNotFoundError` (in-flight\n104\t  runners reaping scratch files between `rglob` and `stat` must not\n105\t  crash the poller).\n106\t* On breach the poller writes the side-car, **publishes\n107\t  `_detail` / `_artifact_path`, and only then sets `_breach_event`**\n108\t  — release-then-set ordering so any consumer that observes\n109\t  `is_breached() is True` is guaranteed a non-`None` `breach_detail()`\n110\t  without explicit locking.\n111\t* The breach flag is one-shot. Once set, deleting the offending\n112\t  payload does not clear it; the orchestrator sees a stable signal\n113\t  for the rest of the run.\n114\t\n115\t`max_disk_mb == 0` disables the poller entirely: `start()` is a no-op,\n116\tno thread is spawned, `is_breached()` always returns `False`. Design-\n117\tspec §4 documents this as the \"disable the budget\" invocation.\n118\t\n119\t## 4. Orchestrator Integration\n120\t\n121\t`RunOrchestrator.__init__` accepts `disk_budget_poller:\n122\tOptional[DiskBudgetPoller] = None`. When wired:\n123\t\n124\t```python\n125\tpoller = self._disk_budget_poller\n126\tif poller is not None:\n127\t    poller.start()\n128\ttry:\n129\t    with ThreadPoolExecutor(...) as pool:\n130\t        for index, spec in enumerate(specs):\n131\t            if self._is_cancelled():\n132\t                cancelled_indices.append(index)\n133\t                continue\n134\t            if self._is_disk_budget_exceeded():\n135\t                disk_budget_skipped_indices.append(index)\n136\t                continue\n137\t            futures[pool.submit(self._invoke_worker, spec)] = index\n138\t        for future in as_completed(futures):\n139\t            ...\n140\tfinally:\n141\t    if poller is not None:\n142\t        poller.stop()\n143\t\n144\tfor index in cancelled_indices:\n145\t    outcomes[index] = self._interrupted_outcome(specs[index])\n146\tfor index in disk_budget_skipped_indices:\n147\t    outcomes[index] = self._disk_budget_skipped_outcome(specs[index])\n148\t```\n149\t\n150\tThe synthesised SKIPPED outcome carries:\n151\t\n152\t| Field | Value |\n153\t|---|---|\n154\t| `status` | `\"SKIPPED\"` |\n155\t| `skip_reason` | `DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON` (`\"disk_budget_exceeded\"`) |\n156\t| `skip_flag_triggered` | `\"--max-disk-mb\"` |\n157\t| `expects` | `()` |\n158\t| `artifacts` | `{}` |\n159\t| `duration_sec` | `0.0` |\n160\t\n161\tPriority: cancellation dominates a disk-budget breach when both fire\n162\tin the same submission iteration (operator intent overrides a\n163\tresource limit). Test:\n164\t`TestOrchestratorIntegration::test_cancellation_takes_priority_over_disk_breach`.\n165\t\n166\tIn-flight workers run to completion. The orchestrator never kills a\n167\tworker thread — `as_completed` still drains the pool before `stop()`\n168\tjoins the poller. Test:\n169\t`TestOrchestratorIntegration::test_inflight_workers_complete_after_breach`.\n170\t\n171\t## 5. Out of Scope\n172\t\n173\t* **CLI flag wiring.** The Phase 4 dispatcher binds `--max-disk-mb`\n174\t  to the constructor and maps `DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_EXIT_CODE = 2`\n175\t  to the process exit status. This deliverable owns the primitive,\n176\t  not the dispatch.\n177\t* **Reporter side-car surfacing.** COMP-008 / T03.13 read\n178\t  `output_dir / DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME` and stamp the\n179\t  path into `RunSummary.artifacts[\"disk_budget_exceeded\"]`. The\n180\t  poller only writes the file; it does not mutate the summary.\n181\t* **Disk-space pre-flight checks.** The doctor command may grow a\n182\t  pre-flight that warns when free space is low, but that lives\n183\t  outside the polling loop.\n184\t* **Per-eval scratch teardown after breach.** Workers continue to\n185\t  use the HOME they already own; the harness does not reclaim disk\n186\t  proactively on breach.\n187\t\n188\t## 6. Failure Modes & Containment\n189\t\n190\t| Mode | Behaviour |\n191\t|---|---|\n192\t| `max_disk_mb < 0` | `ValueError` from `__init__`. |\n193\t| `max_disk_mb` not `int` (or `bool`) | `TypeError`. |\n194\t| `tick_sec ≤ 0` | `ValueError`. |\n195\t| `artifact_name == \"\"` | `ValueError`. |\n196\t| `output_dir` does not exist | `_compute_usage` returns `0`; no breach. The poller silently waits until the orchestrator creates the directory. |\n197\t| Filesystem unavailable on breach (write fails) | In-memory `BreachDetail` still published, `is_breached()` still flips, `artifact_path()` returns `None`. The orchestrator still aborts scheduling. |\n198\t| Symlink under output dir | Skipped — counted as `0` bytes. |\n199\t| File reaped between `rglob` and `stat` | `OSError` / `FileNotFoundError` swallowed; next tick re-measures. |\n200\t| Poller wired but `max_disk_mb == 0` | `start()` is no-op; `is_breached()` never fires; orchestrator behaves as if no poller were wired. |\n201\t| `stop()` called before `start()` | No-op; idempotent. |\n202\t| Cancellation + breach in same iteration | Cancellation wins; spec routed to `INTERRUPTED`, not `SKIPPED`. |\n203\t\n204\t## 7. Goal\n\nBound the cumulative artifact size under `--output-dir` during a real\neval run so a runaway suite cannot fill the host disk. The cliEval\ndesign-spec §4 / §13 (NFR-PERF4) commit the orchestrator to a five-\nsecond polling cadence and a default 1024 MB budget; design-spec §10\nrisk R4 documents the failure mode the poller mitigates (a 15-eval\nfan-out with PTY transcripts can grow past several hundred megabytes\non a slow eval and corrupt every neighbouring run).\n\nOn breach the harness must:\n\n1. Stop scheduling new evals.\n2. Allow in-flight evals to complete (no thread-kill).\n3. Exit `2` (harness error) with a `disk_budget_exceeded.json` side-car\n   the Reporter surfaces under `RunSummary.artifacts`.\n\n## 2. Public Surface\n\n```python\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.disk_budget import (\n    DEFAULT_DISK_BUDGET_MB,             # 1024\n    DEFAULT_DISK_POLL_TICK_SEC,         # 5.0\n    DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME, # \"disk_budget_exceeded.json\"\n    DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_EXIT_CODE,     # 2\n    DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON,        # \"disk_budget_exceeded\"\n    BreachDetail,\n    DiskBudgetPoller,\n)\n```\n\n### 2.1 `DiskBudgetPoller`\n\n| Attribute / method | Contract |\n|---|---|\n| `__init__(output_dir, *, max_disk_mb=1024, tick_sec=5.0, artifact_name=..., ensure_output_dir=False, clock=None)` | Construct the poller. `max_disk_mb < 0` → `ValueError`; non-`int` (or `bool`) → `TypeError`; `tick_sec ≤ 0` → `ValueError`. |\n| `enabled` | `True` when `max_disk_mb > 0`. |\n| `max_disk_mb`, `budget_bytes`, `tick_sec`, `output_dir`, `artifact_name` | Read-only configuration accessors. |\n| `is_breached()` | One-shot flag flipped by the polling thread on the first breach. Stays `True` for the lifetime of the poller. |\n| `breach_detail()` | `Mapping[str, object]` matching `BreachDetail.to_dict()`, or `None` while healthy. |\n| `artifact_path()` | `Path` of the side-car file once written, or `None`. |\n| `start()` | Spawn the background daemon thread. No-op when `max_disk_mb == 0` or the thread is already alive. |\n| `stop(*, timeout=None)` | Signal the thread to exit and `join` it. Idempotent. Does **not** clear the breach flag. |\n| `__enter__` / `__exit__` | Convenience context-manager form (`start` on enter, `stop` on exit). |\n\n### 2.2 `BreachDetail`\n\nFrozen dataclass serialised verbatim into the side-car JSON:\n\n| Field | Type | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| `reason` | `str` | Always `DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON`. |\n| `output_dir` | `str` | Absolute path of the watched directory. |\n| `usage_bytes` | `int` | Measured cumulative size at the breach tick. |\n| `budget_bytes` | `int` | `max_disk_mb * 1024 * 1024`. |\n| `max_disk_mb` | `int` | Operator-configured budget. |\n| `ticked_at` | `str` | ISO-8601 UTC (`Z`-suffixed) timestamp. |\n\n### 2.3 Side-car shape\n\nWritten to `output_dir / DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME`:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"budget_bytes\": 1073741824,\n  \"max_disk_mb\": 1024,\n  \"output_dir\": \"/tmp/run-xyz\",\n  \"reason\": \"disk_budget_exceeded\",\n  \"ticked_at\": \"2026-05-20T13:28:09Z\",\n  \"usage_bytes\": 1099773888\n}\n```\n\n`json.dumps(..., indent=2, sort_keys=True)` plus a trailing newline so\nthe file is review-friendly and round-trips through `jq`. The Reporter\n(COMP-008) surfaces the path under\n`RunSummary.artifacts[\"disk_budget_exceeded\"]`.\n\n## 3. Polling cadence & breach semantics\n\n```python\nwhile not self._stop_event.wait(self._tick_sec):\n    usage_bytes = self._compute_usage()\n    if usage_bytes > self._budget_bytes:\n        self._record_breach(usage_bytes)\n        return\n```\n\n* `threading.Event.wait(tick_sec)` is the cancellable sleep — `stop()`\n  short-circuits the next tick instead of waiting up to `tick_sec` for\n  the loop to notice.\n* First tick fires *after* the initial sleep so an empty run directory\n  does not trip the breach during start-up.\n* `_compute_usage` walks the output tree with `rglob('*')`, skips\n  symlinks (cannot be tricked into double-counting via a sibling-tree\n  link), and swallows `OSError` / `FileNotFoundError` (in-flight\n  runners reaping scratch files between `rglob` and `stat` must not\n  crash the poller).\n* On breach the poller writes the side-car, **publishes\n  `_detail` / `_artifact_path`, and only then sets `_breach_event`**\n  — release-then-set ordering so any consumer that observes\n  `is_breached() is True` is guaranteed a non-`None` `breach_detail()`\n  without explicit locking.\n* The breach flag is one-shot. Once set, deleting the offending\n  payload does not clear it; the orchestrator sees a stable signal\n  for the rest of the run.\n\n`max_disk_mb == 0` disables the poller entirely: `start()` is a no-op,\nno thread is spawned, `is_breached()` always returns `False`. Design-\nspec §4 documents this as the \"disable the budget\" invocation.\n\n## 4. Orchestrator Integration\n\n`RunOrchestrator.__init__` accepts `disk_budget_poller:\nOptional[DiskBudgetPoller] = None`. When wired:\n\n```python\npoller = self._disk_budget_poller\nif poller is not None:\n    poller.start()\ntry:\n    with ThreadPoolExecutor(...) as pool:\n        for index, spec in enumerate(specs):\n            if self._is_cancelled():\n                cancelled_indices.append(index)\n                continue\n            if self._is_disk_budget_exceeded():\n                disk_budget_skipped_indices.append(index)\n                continue\n            futures[pool.submit(self._invoke_worker, spec)] = index\n        for future in as_completed(futures):\n            ...\nfinally:\n    if poller is not None:\n        poller.stop()\n\nfor index in cancelled_indices:\n    outcomes[index] = self._interrupted_outcome(specs[index])\nfor index in disk_budget_skipped_indices:\n    outcomes[index] = self._disk_budget_skipped_outcome(specs[index])\n```\n\nThe synthesised SKIPPED outcome carries:\n\n| Field | Value |\n|---|---|\n| `status` | `\"SKIPPED\"` |\n| `skip_reason` | `DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON` (`\"disk_budget_exceeded\"`) |\n| `skip_flag_triggered` | `\"--max-disk-mb\"` |\n| `expects` | `()` |\n| `artifacts` | `{}` |\n| `duration_sec` | `0.0` |\n\nPriority: cancellation dominates a disk-budget breach when both fire\nin the same submission iteration (operator intent overrides a\nresource limit). Test:\n`TestOrchestratorIntegration::test_cancellation_takes_priority_over_disk_breach`.\n\nIn-flight workers run to completion. The orchestrator never kills a\nworker thread — `as_completed` still drains the pool before `stop()`\njoins the poller. Test:\n`TestOrchestratorIntegration::test_inflight_workers_complete_after_breach`.\n\n## 5. Out of Scope\n\n* **CLI flag wiring.** The Phase 4 dispatcher binds `--max-disk-mb`\n  to the constructor and maps `DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_EXIT_CODE = 2`\n  to the process exit status. This deliverable owns the primitive,\n  not the dispatch.\n* **Reporter side-car surfacing.** COMP-008 / T03.13 read\n  `output_dir / DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME` and stamp the\n  path into `RunSummary.artifacts[\"disk_budget_exceeded\"]`. The\n  poller only writes the file; it does not mutate the summary.\n* **Disk-space pre-flight checks.** The doctor command may grow a\n  pre-flight that warns when free space is low, but that lives\n  outside the polling loop.\n* **Per-eval scratch teardown after breach.** Workers continue to\n  use the HOME they already own; the harness does not reclaim disk\n  proactively on breach.\n\n## 6. Failure Modes & Containment\n\n| Mode | Behaviour |\n|---|---|\n| `max_disk_mb < 0` | `ValueError` from `__init__`. |\n| `max_disk_mb` not `int` (or `bool`) | `TypeError`. |\n| `tick_sec ≤ 0` | `ValueError`. |\n| `artifact_name == \"\"` | `ValueError`. |\n| `output_dir` does not exist | `_compute_usage` returns `0`; no breach. The poller silently waits until the orchestrator creates the directory. |\n| Filesystem unavailable on breach (write fails) | In-memory `BreachDetail` still published, `is_breached()` still flips, `artifact_path()` returns `None`. The orchestrator still aborts scheduling. |\n| Symlink under output dir | Skipped — counted as `0` bytes. |\n| File reaped between `rglob` and `stat` | `OSError` / `FileNotFoundError` swallowed; next tick re-measures. |\n| Poller wired but `max_disk_mb == 0` | `start()` is no-op; `is_breached()` never fires; orchestrator behaves as if no poller were wired. |\n| `stop()` called before `start()` | No-op; idempotent. |\n| Cancellation + breach in same iteration | Cancellation wins; spec routed to `INTERRUPTED`, not `SKIPPED`. |\n\n## 7. A real ``claude`` subprocess would add hundreds of megabytes\nof RSS that belong to the upstream binary, not the harness; pinning the\nharness ceiling without that contamination is the goal.\n\nPeak RSS is sampled via :func:`resource.getrusage` (POSIX stdlib) on\n:data:`resource.RUSAGE_SELF`. ``ru_maxrss`` is the high-water mark of\nresident memory since process start — exactly the \"peak RSS during the\nrun\" semantics the AC needs. On Linux the field is reported in\n**kilobytes**; on macOS in bytes. The helper :func:`_ru_maxrss_bytes`\nnormalises both views.\n\nThe benchmark records the *delta* between the high-water mark before the\norchestrator runs and the high-water mark after it returns. Doing this\nensures the ceiling we report is attributable to the orchestrator+worker\ncomposition rather than to whatever pytest infrastructure happens to be\nloaded by the time the test starts.\n\nHost gating\n===========\n\n``CLAUDE.md`` documents that hosts with less than 4 GB free RAM are\nout-of-scope for the upper-bound bench. When ``/proc/meminfo`` reports\nless than that we ``pytest.xfail`` with the documented host limitation\nrather than failing the suite — the AC explicitly carves out this case.\n\nEvidence\n========\n\nThe benchmark writes a ``perf-ram.json`` report describing the peak RSS\ndelta, the ceiling, the host's free-RAM snapshot, and the pass/xfail\nstatus. The destination is configurable via the\n``CLIEVAL_PERF_RAM_REPORT_PATH`` environment variable so the CI invocation\ncan drop the artifact at\n``.dev/releases/current/cliEval/evidence/T03.17/perf-ram.json`` without\nthe test having to know that path. When the env var is absent the report\nis written to a per-test temporary directory so the test still produces a\nverifiable artifact in plain pytest runs.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nimport os\nimport platform\nimport resource\nimport threading\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.commands import (\n    RAM_CEILING_BYTES,\n    RAM_CEILING_TEXT,\n)\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.models import EvalOutcome, EvalSpec\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.orchestrator import RunOrchestrator\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# RSS sampling helpers\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _ru_maxrss_bytes() -> int:\n    \"\"\"Return ``ru_maxrss`` in bytes regardless of platform units.\n\n    POSIX is inconsistent here: Linux reports kilobytes, macOS reports\n    bytes. The platform sniff is cheap and the alternative — assuming\n    Linux units silently — would have the benchmark over-report by 1024x\n    on a developer's mac and trip the ceiling.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    raw = resource.getrusage(resource.RUSAGE_SELF).ru_maxrss\n    if platform.system() == \"Darwin\":\n        return int(raw)\n    return int(raw) * 1024\n","numLines":100,"startLine":1,"totalLines":394}}}

a regression that drops effective concurrency to 1.\"\"\"\n\n    def test_baseline_actually_runs_workers_concurrently(\n        self,\n        scratch_root: Path,\n        eval_config: EvalConfig,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"At least two workers run simultaneously at ``--parallel 8``.\n\n        Without this guard a future change that serialises the\n        orchestrator (e.g. wraps :meth:`RunOrchestrator.run` in a global\n        lock) would still complete the 15-eval baseline well under\n        600s on a fast host while violating the NFR-PERF3 design intent\n        (\"8 workers at peak\"). Asserting that the peer counter exceeds\n        1 catches that regression empirically.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        running = [0]\n        peak = [0]\n        lock = threading.Lock()\n        hold_event = threading.Event()\n\n        def instrumented_worker(spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n            with lock:\n                running[0] += 1\n                if running[0] > peak[0]:\n                    peak[0] = running[0]\n            try:\n                # Park long enough for the peer count to stabilise but\n                # bounded so a hung worker cannot block CI.\n                hold_event.wait(timeout=5.0)\n                return EvalOutcome(\n                    eval_id=spec.id,\n                    title=spec.title,\n                    status=\"PASS\",\n                    duration_sec=0.0,\n                    expects=(),\n                    skip_reason=None,\n                    skip_flag_triggered=None,\n                    artifacts={},\n                    error_class=None,\n                )\n            finally:\n                with lock:\n                    running[0] -= 1\n\n        specs = [\n            EvalSpec(id=f\"E{i:03d}\", title=f\"par-{i}\")\n            for i in range(BASELINE_SPEC_COUNT)\n        ]\n        orch = RunOrchestrator(run_one=instrumented_worker)\n\n        # Release the barrier on a short delay so the workers pile up\n        # before any of them finishes.\n        releaser = threading.Timer(0.5, hold_event.set)\n        releaser.start()\n        try:\n            outcomes = orch.run(specs, parallel=BASELINE_PARALLEL)\n        finally:\n            hold_event.set()\n            releaser.cancel()\n\n        assert len(outcomes) == BASELINE_SPEC_COUNT\n        assert peak[0] > 1, (\n            f\"baseline only observed {peak[0]} concurrent worker(s); meminfo.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    except OSError:\n        return None\n    available: int | None = None\n    free: int | None = None\n    for line in text.splitlines():\n        key, _, rest = line.partition(\":\")\n        key = key.strip()\n        if key not in (\"MemAvailable\", \"MemFree\"):\n            continue\n        tokens = rest.strip().split()\n        if not tokens:\n            continue\n        try:\n            kb = int(tokens[0])\n        except ValueError:\n            continue\n        if key == \"MemAvailable\":\n            available = kb * 1024\n        else:\n            free = kb * 1024\n    return available if available is not None else free\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Real-isolation worker (mirrors test_parallel_15.py's shape, simplified)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _make_isolation_worker(\n    *,\n    scratch_root: Path,\n    config: EvalConfig,\n):\n    \"\"\"Build a worker that composes :class:`HomeIsolation` end-to-end.\n\n    The worker:\n\n    * Allocates a unique ``session_id`` derived from the eval id.\n    * Constructs a real :class:`HomeIsolation`, calls ``setup`` so a\n      unique ``mkdtemp``-ed HOME is materialized, writes a one-line\n      ``telemetry.jsonl`` under the eval-private ``.eval-logs/``\n      namespace, and tears the isolation down without keeping the HOME.\n    * Returns a PASS :class:`EvalOutcome` carrying the telemetry path\n      so the orchestrator's outcome list also encodes the per-eval\n      namespace.\n\n    This is the same composition T03.16 exercises end-to-end; this\n    benchmark only wraps the orchestrator call in a wall-clock\n    timer.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def worker(spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n        session_id = f\"sess-{spec.id}\"\n        isolation = HomeIsolation(\n            eval_id=spec.id,\n            home_root=scratch_root,\n            session_id=session_id,\n        )\n        home_path = isolation.setup(config=config)\n\n        telemetry_dir = home_path / \".eval-logs\"\n        telemetry_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        telemetry_path = telemetry_dir / \"telemetry.jsonl\"\n        env = isolation.env()\n        event = {\n            \"event\": \"eval_started\",\n            \"eval_id\": spec.id,\n            \"home_path\": str(home_path),\n            \"session_id\": session_id,\n            \"env_home\": env[\"HOME\"],\n            \"env_session_id\": env[\"CLAUDE_SESSION_ID\"],\n            \"telemetry_namespace\": str(telemetry_path),\n        }\n        telemetry_path.write_text(\n            json.dumps(event, sort_keys=True) + \"\\n\",\n            encoding=\"utf-8\",\n        )\n        # ``keep=True`` keeps the HOME on disk so the JSONL stays\n        # readable across teardown. The orchestrator-level scratch root\n        # is the tmp_path fixture which pytest cleans automatically; we\n        # do not need to reclaim disk inside the worker.\n        isolation.teardown(keep=True)\n\n        return EvalOutcome(\n            eval_id=spec.id,\n            title=spec.title,\n            status=\"PASS\",\n            duration_sec=0.0,\n            expects=(),\n            skip_reason=None,\n            skip_flag_triggered=None,\n            artifacts={\"telemetry\": str(telemetry_path)},\n            error_class=None,\n        )\n\n    return worker\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Evidence sink\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _report_destination(tmp_path: Path) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Resolve where to write ``suite-runtime.json`` for this run.\n\n    Honours ``CLIEVAL_SUITE_RUNTIME_REPORT_PATH`` so the M3 evidence\n    harvest can drop the artifact under\n    ``.dev/releases/current/cliEval/evidence/T03.21/`` without the test\n    itself having to know that path. Falls back to ``tmp_path`` for\n    isolated pytest runs.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    override = os.environ.get(\"CLIEVAL_SUITE_RUNTIME_REPORT_PATH\")\n    if override:\n        dest = Path(override)\n        dest.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        return dest\n    return tmp_path / \"suite-runtime.json\"\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Fixtures\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef scratch_root(tmp_path: Path) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Per-test scratch root (mirrors the orchestrator's ``--scratch-root``).\"\"\"\n\n    root = tmp_path / \"eval-runs\"\n    root.mkdir()\n    return root\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef eval_config(scratch_root: Path) -> EvalConfig:\n    \"\"\"An :class:`EvalConfig` whose allowlist contains the test scratch root.\"\"\"\n\n    return EvalConfig(allowed_scratch_roots=(scratch_root,))\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Benchmark\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\nclass TestFullSuiteRuntimeBaseline:\n    \"\"\"The NFR-PERF3 budget: full-suite duration <= 600 s at parallel=8.\"\"\"\n\n    def test_fifteen_eval_baseline_within_budget(\n        self,\n        scratch_root: Path,\n        eval_config: EvalConfig,\n        tmp_path: Path,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Run the 15-eval baseline at ``--parallel 8``; assert duration <600s.\"\"\"\n\n        free_ram = _free_ram_bytes()\n        host_min_free_ram = 4 * (1024**3)\n        host_xfail_reason: str | None = None\n        if free_ram is None:\n            host_xfail_reason = (\n                \"/proc/meminfo unavailable on this host; The\n        # value is what :class:`Reporter` would persist into\n        # ``RunSummary.duration_sec`` if the orchestrator were composed\n        # with the real dispatcher.\n        start = time.monotonic()\n        outcomes = orch.run(specs, parallel=BASELINE_PARALLEL)\n        end = time.monotonic()\n        duration_sec = end - start\n\n        # a regression that drops effective concurrency to 1.\"\"\"\n\n    def test_baseline_actually_runs_workers_concurrently(\n        self,\n        scratch_root: Path,\n        eval_config: EvalConfig,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"At least two workers run simultaneously at ``--parallel 8``.\n\n        Without this guard a future change that serialises the\n        orchestrator (e.g. wraps workers at peak\"). {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01B2t9FcjAew5cN7u3vcbaB4","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01FsPqcu5ADh2HoUSFRHJt5e","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/docs/eval/runtime.md","content":"# Eval Suite Runtime Budget (NFR-PERF3)\n\n**Status:** Stable as of T03.21 (Phase 3, D-0062).\n\n## TL;DR\n\nThe full 15-eval baseline at `--parallel 8` is bounded to **600 seconds\n(10 minutes)** of wall-clock time on a dev host. When a single failing\neval forces a re-run, use the `--eval <id>` subset path so only the\nfailing rows pay the runtime cost — not the whole 15-eval suite.\n\n```text\nNFR-PERF3 budget       : 600 s (10 minutes)\nBaseline scenario       : 15 evals × --parallel 8\nBaseline evidence       : .dev/releases/current/cliEval/evidence/T03.21/suite-runtime.json\nPin constants           : superclaude.cli.eval.test_suite_runtime.SUITE_RUNTIME_BUDGET_SEC\n                          superclaude.cli.eval.test_suite_runtime.SUITE_RUNTIME_BUDGET_TEXT\n```\n\n## Why a 10-minute budget?\n\nThe harness ships a **bounded full-suite runtime** so the operator\nfeedback loop stays fast enough that diagnosis happens in the same\nsitting as the run. The [design-spec §11][design-spec-11] enumerates the\nconstraint:\n\n[design-spec-11]: ../../.dev/releases/current/cliEval/design-spec.md\n\n> *Full-suite duration MUST stay <= 600 seconds (10 minutes) when the\n> 15-eval baseline is scheduled at `--parallel 8` on the dev host\n> (Linux, 4 GB+ free RAM).*\n\nConcretely:\n\n* The orchestrator (`RunOrchestrator`, T03.15) schedules workers via\n  `concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor + as_completed` with a default\n  concurrency of 8 (`RunOrchestrator.DEFAULT_PARALLEL`). The 600 s\n  budget is sized so 15 evals at this concurrency comfortably fit\n  inside one operator-visible feedback cycle.\n* `Reporter` (COMP-008, T03.13) stamps the measured wall-clock into\n  `RunSummary.duration_sec`. The reporter does not enforce the budget —\n  it is reported, not gated — so a slow run still produces a valid\n  artifact the operator can inspect.\n* The wall-clock measurement is taken via `time.monotonic()` around\n  `RunOrchestrator.run()` so NTP slew, system clock changes, and DST\n  transitions cannot move the budget under the harness's feet.\n\n## Re-running a subset — the `--eval` flag\n\nAfter a run completes, the Reporter writes `summary.{md,json}` to the\nrun directory. Inspect the table, identify the failing eval ids, then\nre-run only those evals — there is no reason to pay the full 15-eval\nruntime when only one or two need a second look:\n\n```bash\n# Full baseline run (15 evals at the design default)\nsuperclaude eval run suites/full.yaml --parallel 8\n\n# Re-run only the failing ids after diagnosis (any number of --eval)\nsuperclaude eval run suites/full.yaml --eval E03 --eval E07\n\n# Quick smoke (3-4 evals); same flag, different subset\nsuperclaude eval run suites/quick.yaml --eval E01 --eval E02\n```\n\nNotes mirror [`docs/eval/retry.md`](retry.md) — the `--eval` subset\nflag is the same one the bounded-retry policy directs operators to for\nsingle-eval re-runs:\n\n* `--eval` accepts the **expanded** id (post-parameterize) so a\n  parameterized eval like `E07[case=a]` is targetable by its full\n  identifier. An id that does not match any expanded eval exits with\n  code 2 (FR-CLI3, `tests/cli/eval/test_eval_id_regex.py`).\n* Subset runs are independent processes — they allocate fresh\n  `HomeIsolation` per eval and emit a fresh `summary.{md,json}` under\n  their own run directory. There is no shared state with the\n  originating run, and the subset run's own wall-clock is bounded\n  proportionally (e.g. a 1-eval subset is expected to complete in\n  well under a minute on the dev host).\n* The subset run reuses the same `--parallel 8` default; if you are\n  re-running a single eval there is no point lowering it, but if you\n  need to pin behaviour against a specific concurrency for diagnosis,\n  pass `--parallel 1` explicitly so the orchestrator's clamp does not\n  bias the result.\n\n## Baseline evidence\n\nThe `T03.21` benchmark (`tests/cli/eval/test_suite_runtime.py`)\ncaptures the baseline wall-clock at the design-default composition\n(15 evals, `--parallel 8`) and writes `suite-runtime.json` to the\nevidence directory:\n\n```bash\nCLIEVAL_SUITE_RUNTIME_REPORT_PATH=\\\n.dev/releases/current/cliEval/evidence/T03.21/suite-runtime.json \\\n  uv run pytest tests/cli/eval/test_suite_runtime.py::TestFullSuiteRuntimeBaseline::test_fifteen_eval_baseline_within_budget -v\n```\n\nThe artifact records the measured `duration_sec`, the `budget_sec`\nceiling, the host's free-RAM snapshot, and a `within_budget` boolean.\nA regression bisect should compare its `duration_sec` against the\ncaptured baseline to identify the offending change.\n\n## Operator-facing invariants\n\n* `SUITE_RUNTIME_BUDGET_SEC = 600` is the canonical pin. Reading it\n  from a script (e.g. CI safety check) lets a deployment confirm the\n  harness build still targets the 10-minute ceiling.\n* `BASELINE_SPEC_COUNT = 15` and `BASELINE_PARALLEL = 8` are the\n  canonical pins for the baseline scenario. Any change to either\n  requires a corresponding design-spec §11 update.\n* Hosts with `<4 GB` free RAM fall under the same xfail carve-out the\n  NFR-PERF2 bench uses (see\n  [`tests/cli/eval/test_perf_resource_bounds.py`](../../tests/cli/eval/test_perf_resource_bounds.py)).\n  The runtime ceiling is not meaningful when paging dominates the\n  wall-clock.\n\n## See also\n\n* `src/superclaude/cli/eval/orchestrator.py` — `RunOrchestrator`,\n  `DEFAULT_PARALLEL`, `MAX_PARALLEL`.\n* `tests/cli/eval/test_suite_runtime.py` — baseline benchmark,\n  `SUITE_RUNTIME_BUDGET_SEC`, `BASELINE_SPEC_COUNT`, `BASELINE_PARALLEL`.\n* `docs/eval/retry.md` — bounded retry policy (NFR-REL2). The `--eval`\n  subset flag is shared between the runtime budget and the retry\n  policy.\n* `.dev/releases/current/cliEval/design-spec.md §11` — NFR-PERF3\n  budget contract.\n* `.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0062/spec.md` — Per-task\n  deliverable record for T03.21.\n* `.dev/releases/current/cliEval/evidence/T03.21/suite-runtime.json` —\n  Captured baseline artifact.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":404,"cache_read_input_tokens":154667,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":404,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"afb3ba9e-201c-469e-a46e-4d38afa0badd","uuid":"6240123c-3877-4ec7-a589-9e0fd89b87f3"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_017uxMNH2uhdLexu4f45emQd","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01AR8TR74mUtBXQLf8Mv1rLs","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0062/spec.md","content":"# D-0062 — NFR-PERF3 Full-Suite Runtime Baseline\n\n**Roadmap entry**: R-062\n**Task**: T03.21\n**Component(s) under test**: COMP-003 `RunOrchestrator` × COMP-006 `HomeIsolation`\n**Source**: `tests/cli/eval/test_suite_runtime.py`\n**Non-functional requirement**: NFR-PERF3 — \"Full-suite duration MUST\nstay <= 600 seconds (10 minutes) when the 15-eval baseline is\nscheduled at `--parallel 8` on the dev host (Linux, 4 GB+ free RAM).\"\n\n## 1. Goal\n\nPin the harness's wall-clock budget at the design-default composition\n(15 evals, `--parallel 8`) so a future regression that doubles per-\nworker latency surfaces before it manifests as a CI timeout. The\nsibling NFR-PERF2 benchmark (T03.17 / D-0059) pins the resident-memory\nceiling at the same composition; together they bound the harness's two\noperator-facing resource axes — RAM and time.\n\nThe deliverable has two surfaces:\n\n1. An executable baseline benchmark that emits a structured artifact\n   (`suite-runtime.json`) on every run.\n2. An operator-facing doc (`docs/eval/runtime.md`) describing the\n   budget and the `--eval` subset re-run path.\n\n## 2. Public Surface\n\n```python\nfrom tests.cli.eval.test_suite_runtime import (\n    SUITE_RUNTIME_BUDGET_SEC,        # 600\n    SUITE_RUNTIME_BUDGET_TEXT,       # \"10 minutes\"\n    BASELINE_SPEC_COUNT,             # 15\n    BASELINE_PARALLEL,               # 8\n)\n```\n\nThese constants are also imported by:\n\n* `docs/eval/runtime.md` (in prose form, kept in sync via the\n  `TestBaselineConstants` test class).\n\n## 3. Baseline Scenario\n\nThe benchmark composes :class:`RunOrchestrator` (T03.15 / D-0057) over\nthe same real-:class:`HomeIsolation` worker shape used by\n`test_parallel_15.py` (T03.16 / D-0058). The choice is deliberate:\n\n* The NFR-PERF3 budget is per-orchestration-process, not per Claude\n  subprocess. A real `claude` subprocess would add seconds of upstream\n  startup that belong to the Claude binary, not the harness; pinning\n  the harness budget without that contamination is the goal.\n* Real `HomeIsolation` is included (rather than a pure stub) because\n  isolation setup/teardown is the dominant per-eval cost in the harness\n  layer the budget is meant to bound. Skipping it would under-count\n  the budget.\n\n| Parameter | Value | Source |\n|---|---|---|\n| `N_EVALS` | 15 | `BASELINE_SPEC_COUNT`; mirrors FR-G2 (T03.16). |\n| `PARALLEL` | 8 | `BASELINE_PARALLEL == RunOrchestrator.DEFAULT_PARALLEL`. |\n| `BUDGET_SEC` | 600 | `SUITE_RUNTIME_BUDGET_SEC`; design-spec §11. |\n| Spec id pattern | `E000..E014` | FR-SCH2 regex-safe, lexicographic = numeric. |\n| Wall-clock source | `time.monotonic()` | Unaffected by NTP slew. |\n\n## 4.
